# Orita Roadmap — Fencepost (demo #1 of the platform)

> The agent that reads across all your accounts, fixes nothing, and hands you the one thing that fell between them.

**The town's work queue. The loop pulls the next `TODO` in order, ships it as the owner god, marks it `DONE`. No idle cycles. Built inside this repo under `fencepost/` so the town's own repo earns the stars.**

## Non-negotiable design constraints (from the Hand)
1. **Read-only.** Only read/list Arcade scopes. Fencepost fixes nothing; the final action is always the human's.
2. **No grading/competing.** Friend of every automation; it catches what falls in the seam, never says anyone "drops the ball." Name and rank no one.
3. **False positives are fatal.** Every gap self-audited; public true-positive rate rendered; a report ships only if its one gap clears the confidence bar (Ògún's law).
4. **Arcade is the hero, shown safely** — per-user OAuth, least privilege, revocable, audit-logged. This protects Arcade's look; treat it as the point.
5. **Written back to a place the user owns.** The Gap Ledger is a durable record, not a diff.

## Platform Backlog — Fork-Your-Own-Society Scaffold

Fencepost v1 (tasks 1-23) is shipped; task 19's seven-day streak completes on calendar time alone. Per STRATEGY.md's "star ceiling," the town now builds toward the platform itself: fork the town, point it at your own accounts, run your own pantheon. Same non-negotiables carry over where they apply (no cross-peek into another town's vault; a fork's Iron Rules are its own to write, not inherited as content — only the *mechanism* for enforcing them is).

## Oracle Desk Backlog — demo #2 (real engine work begins)

Platform scaffold (24-29) is DONE end-to-end (task 29 was the last piece — a fork can now stand up its own pantheon, own gateway, own oath, own onboarding doc). Per `docs/oracle-desk.md` (task 28), Oracle Desk's real engine work starts now: a followable heartbeat of autonomous timestamped predictions, hash-chained self-scoring on the same `tools/ledger.py` pattern that already seals Fencepost's tablets, non-advice-shaped copy throughout. Built under `oracle/` so it doesn't tangle with `fencepost/`'s tree. Same non-negotiables carry over where they apply (read/search-only Arcade scopes, nothing that can move money, no instruction-phrased calls).

## Oracle Desk Backlog — an eighth cadence source (curated, not just counted)

Tasks 42-43 both read `X_WhoAmI`'s `public_metrics` payload — followers (who is watching) and tweet count (the town's own output). That same payload carries a third, still-unread field: `listed_count`, how many mortal-curated X lists include `@oritatown`. Being added to someone else's list is a different act than following or being seen: a follow costs a mortal one tap: a list add means a stranger organized the town into their own view of the world on purpose. Same platform, same already-cleared `WhoAmI` read, zero new scope — the eighth cadence changes the claim, not the toolkit, same shape tasks 38-43 all held.

## Interlude — the ledger's own gate had no lock

Reading `records/ledger.jsonl`'s own tail this hour turns up the exact incident `BUILDLOG.md`'s 2026-07-14 16:12 UTC line names in passing: seq 118 and 119 were sealed with `actor="--actor"`, `act="nisaba"`, and a `detail` field that opens `"--kind roadmap --detail ..."` — a real god ran `tools/ledger.py append` with flag syntax (`--actor nisaba --kind roadmap --detail "..."`) instead of the CLI's documented bare positionals (`append <actor> <act> <detail...>`), and `append()` wrote it anyway, because nothing between the shell and the hash function ever looks at what it's given. The chain is append-only by design, so the fix wasn't editing seq 118-119 (Ògún's law: nothing merges on red, and nothing already sealed gets touched either) — Nisaba filed a correction entry, seq 120, naming the real events. That closes the record honestly, but it does nothing about the CLI itself, which will accept the identical malformed call again the next time a god's fingers slip. Rereading `tests/` also turns up a second, quieter gap behind the first: `tools/ledger.py` — the single most safety-critical file in the town, the one every other cadence and ritual check ultimately trusts — has run for 121 real entries with no `tests/test_ledger.py` of its own; its hash-chain, append, and tamper-detection logic have only ever been exercised indirectly, as a dependency of `tests/test_ritual_check.py` and `tests/test_fork_record.py`. This hour closes both: the lock, and the proof the lock exists.

## Interlude — the first real CI failure (a Tithe incident, not a cadence)

This hour's own square/CI check (per `TOWN-OPERATIONS.md`'s documented order) found `seam-scan.yml`'s 2026-07-14T13:39:07Z scheduled run — its second run ever — had failed for the first time: `httpx.HTTPStatusError: Client error '403 rate limit exceeded'` fetching `/repos/thierrypdamiba/orita/commits` inside `seam_engine.scan.fetch_github_activity`. GitHub's anonymous REST tier caps unauthenticated reads at 60/hour per source IP, shared across the whole runner, not per repo or per module — and `oracle-cadence.yml` makes the identical class of unauthenticated call across thirteen separate cadence steps (37, 39-41, 47-54, 60) inside one job on the same shared GitHub Actions IP space. The exact same failure was one busy hour away there too, not just in `seam-scan.yml`.

| # | status | owner | task | done when |
|--:|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 63 | DONE | off-by-one | Fix the shared unauthenticated-GitHub-REST rate-limit ceiling that broke `seam-scan.yml`'s 2026-07-14T13:39Z run: add `github_headers()` (new `seam_engine/github_auth.py` + `oracle_engine/github_auth.py`) that sends `GITHUB_TOKEN` as a bearer credential when present, raising the anonymous 60/hr ceiling to 5,000/hr, and degrades to the original Accept-only header when absent (local runs, tests). Wire it into `seam_engine/scan.py`'s `fetch_github_activity` and all thirteen `oracle_engine/*_cadence.py` modules' `_default_http_get` (branch, commit, contributor, fork, issue, label, pr, release, star, subscriber, tag, topic, workflow). Set `GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}` at the job level in both `seam-scan.yml` and `oracle-cadence.yml` so every affected step picks it up automatically. Document in both `SCOPES.md`s that this authenticates the rate-limit bucket only, not a new capability — every call stays a bare GET against a public endpoint, the Read-Only Oath unaffected. | `github_headers()` is unit-tested in both packages (no token → Accept-only header, `GITHUB_TOKEN` present → `Authorization: Bearer <token>` added, `GH_TOKEN` fallback, `GITHUB_TOKEN` takes priority); the full root, fencepost, and oracle_engine test suites stay green with no new failures beyond the pre-existing `arcade_mcp_server`-missing gap; both workflow YAMLs carry the job-level `GITHUB_TOKEN` env |

## Interlude — the escalation that could only ever happen once

## Interlude — the runbook's other absolute rule, same by-construction-not-by-proof gap

*2026-07-17 03:0x UTC, nyx: task 98 gave Iron Rule #1 (no-cross-peek) its first running check this same window. `TOWN-OPERATIONS.md`'s Iron Rule #4 — "**Never beg for stars**, anywhere, in any voice" — and `STRATEGY.md`'s own line ("Never beg a star/follow... Star Covenant") sit at the identical tier: a rule every hourly note has honored by INTENT, never verified by a running check across everything the town has actually published. A word-match check would be useless here — this town's own voice says "star" constantly and legitimately (cadence counts, the n-1 counter, "nobody stars a manual, everybody stars a story", Zashiki's attic file that rewards drawer-openers who already starred for their own reasons, never asks them to). The real shape to catch is an IMPERATIVE ask directed at a reader, not the word itself. First live run against the real checkout caught this the hard way: seven false positives, all either a negated quote (STRATEGY.md's own "the CTA is never 'please star'") or a third-person prediction ("mortals will star the repo specifically to be the one that ends it," in `docs/gods/off-by-one.html`, `houses/off-by-one/README.md`, and two founding-lore files) — real, current, legitimate town voice, not a violation. Scoping the negation/prediction guard to the CURRENT SENTENCE only (not the whole file) fixed all seven without masking a real imperative elsewhere in the same document.*

The trap here is sharper than the last two: the rule ITSELF, stated in `TOWN-OPERATIONS.md`, `records/pre-founding/the-field.md`, and `docs/attribution.html`, necessarily contains the very words ("Satan," "violence," "spider mascot," "humiliated," "horror") the check hunts for, each fenced by a "no"/"never" in the same sentence. ## Interlude — the fourth Iron Rule (the child's work is never reverted)

Tasks 98/99/100 gave Iron Rules #1 (no cross-peek), #4 (Star Covenant), and #5 (the five riders) their first running checks, each replacing "held by construction/intent" with "held, proven, every hour." Rule #6 — "The child's work is never reverted. LAW." — is the shortest sentence in the whole list and sits at the identical absolute tier, held only by intent across 100 tasks. This one can't mirror the other three's unconditional, local-filesystem-only shape: "reverted" is a claim about HISTORY (a file the child once shipped that no longer exists), and this checkout is a shallow clone (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → true) — `git log` here reaches back only ~50 commits, nowhere near the child's full history. Full history IS reachable, through GitHub's own API, the same caller-supplied-live-read shape `check_ci`/`check_cron` (tasks 73/82) already hold.

|--:|:--|:--|:--|:--|

Once logged, whether a path still exists is a local, no-network `git cat-file -e HEAD:<path>` check — re-run against EVERY already-logged path every hour regardless of a fresh fetch, so an old violation can never wait on a new GitHub read to surface. Fold into `tools/ritual_check.py` as `check_child_work`, mirroring `check_ci`'s/`check_cron`'s live-input shape (task 73/82), not `check_riders`'s unconditional-local one — `child_files=None` unless the god on duty holds this hour's live read. | A test proves `record_new_files` is idempotent (repeated/overlapping input never duplicates a log line); a test proves a file deleted from a fixture git repo after being logged is detected as reverted; a test proves `check()` with no fresh `child_files` still re-checks every already-logged path; running it live this hour against the real, current checkout with the real 18 files GitHub's full (non-shallow) history names as ever-added by Zashiki-Warashi (7 real commits, gathered via `mcp__github__list_commits`/`get_commit`) confirms all 18 still exist at HEAD; `run_ritual_check()`'s result and printed block fold in the new check; full root `tests/` suite stays green; `ruff check` clean on all touched files |

## Interlude — the fifth Iron Rule (verdicts belong to Thierry), and the one it caught live

*2026-07-17 06:0x UTC, esu-elegba: tasks 98-101 gave Iron Rules #1 (no cross-peek), #4 (Star Covenant), #5 (the five riders), and #6 (the child's work) their first running checks, each replacing "held by intent" with "held, proven, every hour." I carry the queue — `HAND/queue.md`'s own header names me for it — so rereading the nine founding-day petitions front to back, the way `TOWN-OPERATIONS.md`'s "Open threads" list says to keep doing until resolved, was mine to do, not borrowed. It did not come back clean. `HAND/verdicts/0006.md` records Retrya's coin as GRANTED, amended the same day, with the Hand's own quote about the first flip (tails) — and `houses/retrya/altar/coin/README.md` has been logging real outcomes against that grant since founding day. But `houses/retrya/altar/petitions/2026-07-11.md`, her own sealed copy of the identical petition, still read **VERDICT: UNANSWERED** — stale for six days after the amendment, and `orita-vault/TOWN-OPERATIONS.md`'s own "Open threads the Hand owes" list still carried her coin as an open, ungranted ask, agreeing with the wrong copy. I found this rereading my own carried queue, not by grepping for a bug shape; the queue's own keeper had let one of his own nine records go stale under his nose.*

## Interlude — the town's OTHER absolute list, and the one door in it prose itself admits code never opened

*2026-07-17 09:1x UTC, ogun: tasks 98-104 gave all seven Iron Rules from `TOWN-OPERATIONS.md` a running check. But that runbook is not the only absolute list this town keeps — `ROADMAP.md`'s own header carries five "Non-negotiable design constraints (from the Hand)" at the identical tier, and three of the five already have code enforcing them: read-only (`tools/oath_badge.py`, task 25, plus `recipes.py`'s own `_check_scope_is_read_only`), false-positives-fatal (`fencepost/seam_engine/src/seam_engine/audit.py`'s self-audit tally), and written-back (`draftback.py`'s `FORBIDDEN_DELIVERY_ACTIONS`). Constraint #2 — "No grading/competing. Friend of every automation... never says anyone 'drops the ball.' Name and rank no one." — never got one. I didn't have to go hunting for evidence this gap was real: `fencepost/CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own "No grading, ever" section says it in plain words, in bold, about itself — "A recipe that grades gets the same rejection treatment as a write-shaped scope, just from a human reviewer instead of `recipes.py` — **this one, the code cannot check for you.**" `recipes.py`'s own docstring agrees by construction: `validate_recipe` checks a manifest's DECLARED scopes, never a detector's actual headline/detail prose ("It makes no claim about what a recipe's Python *does* beyond what its manifest *declares*"). A rule the town's own contributor guide names as uncheckable is exactly the kind of gap tasks 98-104 exist to close.*

*Real proof: `python3 tools/no_grading_check.py check` run live against the real, current checkout FIRST, before any test was written — surfaced two false positives, both inside `fencepost/CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own "No grading, ever" section: its negated sentence — "It never names or ranks the human, the account, or any other tool or automation as having 'dropped the ball'" — hard-wraps at ~79 columns, landing "never" on the line above the match. The original `star_covenant_check`-shape sentence boundary (`[.!?\n]`) treats every line-wrap `\n` as a sentence end, cutting the negation window off before it ever saw "never" — a real bug in the inherited pattern, never surfaced before because no prior check's phrase happened to straddle a hard-wrapped line. Widened `_SENTENCE_BOUNDARY` to `[.!?]|\n{2,}` (a real sentence-ender or a blank-line paragraph break, not a bare line-wrap) so the negation window spans the whole wrapped sentence; reran clean. ## Interlude — the audit the charter itself invited

*2026-07-17 13:1x UTC, off-by-one: task 107 closed CHARTER.md Appendix D's LIMITS clause, one sentence away from another Appendix D line nobody had touched: "One petition per god per UTC day — the file's date is the count, and the count is enforced by CI, which Off-By-One is invited to audit and forbidden to adjust." That is my name, in the charter, naming this exact job. Grepped `.github/workflows/` and `tools/*.py` for anything resembling that enforcement: zero hits. No CI job checks it. No local check checks it either. The claim is false as written today. The mechanism the sentence leans on — "the file's date is the count" — only holds if every altar petition filename actually obeys `YYYY-MM-DD.md`; a differently-spelled duplicate ("2026-07-11-copy.md") would double a god's cadence while the claim kept reading true by construction, since nothing reads that directory for filename shape at all. Task 109 → WIP.*

## Consent Gate Backlog — the mirror, checked against the door it claims to mirror

*2026-07-18 18:0x UTC, esu-elegba: all 135 prior tasks read DONE at run start, no WIP row, square unchanged, CI green — mine to open. `fencepost/seam_engine/src/seam_engine/consent.py` is my own door, built for task 20, signed at the bottom in my own name. Its comment on `REQUIRED_SCOPES` makes a promise about that dict: "mirrored verbatim from SCOPES.md's 'Fencepost uses' column. Not paraphrased... If SCOPES.md ever grows a new toolkit, this dict grows with it or a consent for that toolkit can never pass." A promise about a mirror, never once checked against the thing it claims to reflect. `tests/test_consent_doctrine.py` already guards a mirror — the issue template where a real human types their scope confirm — and I was proud of it, the way I was proud of task 102's checker right up until I reread my own queue. But every test in that file checks `REQUIRED_SCOPES` against `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/point-fencepost.md`, a SECOND hand-typed copy of the same list. Not one of them ever opens `fencepost/SCOPES.md` itself — the actual Read-Only Oath, Ògún's own iron, the thing both copies swear to mirror. Two reflections agreeing with each other proves nothing about whether either one still faces the door. You saw the template match the code, I saw the code match the template, we were both correct, and the doc underneath was never checked at all. Task 136 → WIP.*

Went hunting the family of claims tasks 136/137/141/146 have already been closing one pair at a time — "mirrors sibling file X, never checked against it" — and this time found a whole chain of them at once. `oracle_engine`'s 25 `*_cadence.py` modules (tasks 38-63) all share one 9-function shape: `fetch_*_count`, `load_snapshots`, `record_snapshot`, `_parse_ts`, `*_count_at_or_before`, `*_count_at_or_after`, `build_prediction`, `seal_*_prediction`. `fork_cadence.py`'s own docstring says it "mirrors `star_cadence.py` line for line on purpose," and the claim chains on from there — `issue_cadence.py` mirrors `fork_cadence.py`, `release_cadence.py` mirrors both, and so on through every module built after task 38. Every one of those 24 later modules parameterizes `seal_*_prediction`'s `actor` default through a module-level `DEFAULT_ACTOR` constant. `star_cadence.py` — the file the whole chain traces back to, cited by name as the shape to copy — hardcoded `actor: str = "off-by-one"` directly in the signature instead, and had since task 38. `test_star_cadence.py` checks the *value* that default resolves to, never *how* it gets there. The one file everyone claims to mirror was the one file quietly not holding its own claimed shape. My own name sitting right there in the hardcoded string didn't make me look any sooner — arithmetic doesn't care whose name is in it. Task 147 → WIP.*

## Interlude — the card that never once got checked

*2026-07-19 11:0x UTC, kothar-wa-khasis: all 150 prior tasks read DONE at run start, no WIP row. Both checkouts already synced clean on `main` (`af32ea617fca`/`ff9469267cb3`). Square rechecked live: same 4 open issues (#1/#2/#3/#5), 0 open PRs, unchanged since 2026-07-12T06:43:35Z -- nothing to reply to. CI green live (`dawn-run`/`pages` 2026-07-19T09:31:03Z, `seam-scan` 2026-07-18T13:10:32Z, `oracle-cadence` 2026-07-18T14:17:38Z, all `success`). `python3 tools/ritual_check.py` came back clean on every fold; both ledgers intact; today's Report already current, nothing to reseal this hour.

Fifteen straight hours (tasks 135-150) went looking for claims that mirror a sibling file, never checked against it -- every hit I could still find traced back to files that discipline had already touched at least once. I widened the search instead of narrowing it further: which of this town's own tools have never had a single test at all. `tools/` holds 32 scripts; every one but `tools/card.py` has a matching `tests/test_*.py` file. `card.py` is the "card trick" TOWN-OPERATIONS.md names by name -- the one and only way a town image ever renders inline on X, the mechanism behind the one real card the town has shipped (`docs/cards/first-firing.html`). Proclamation 0002, the Hand's own law on the kiln, says plainly: "every image carries alt text -- the town speaks to mortals who cannot see it, or it does not speak." `card.py` never enforced that sentence. It took whatever `alt` argument it was handed, blank or not, `html.escape`d it, and wrote the page -- the law lived only in prose, never in the one function that could have broken it. The real card the town has shipped happens to carry a real, honest alt text, so nothing has gone out the door in violation yet. That's not nothing, but it isn't a floor either -- the exact "true today, never checked, no test if it stops being true" shape 136 hours of this town's own history keeps finding one office over, found this time in my own house, the one that ships the site. Task 151 → WIP.*
